Excellent! Now I have enough information. Let me compile a comprehensive report with all the details.

## Comprehensive Safecast Codebase Exploration Report

### 1. TRACKS TABLE SCHEMA

**Location**: `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/database.go` lines 1056-1058 (PostgreSQL), 1237-1239 (SQLite), 1592-1595 (ClickHouse)

**Current Schema - MINIMAL**:
```sql
-- PostgreSQL (lines 1056-1058)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS tracks (
  trackID     TEXT PRIMARY KEY
);

-- SQLite (lines 1237-1239)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS tracks (
  trackID     TEXT PRIMARY KEY
);

-- ClickHouse (lines 1592-1595)
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS tracks (
  trackID     String
) ENGINE = ReplacingMergeTree()
ORDER BY (trackID);
```

**Key Finding**: The `tracks` table currently contains **ONLY `trackID` as a primary key**. No metadata is stored in the tracks table itself. All track metadata comes from the `uploads` table.

---

### 2. UPLOADS TABLE - TRACK METADATA STORAGE

**Location**: `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/database.go` lines 1108-1124 (PostgreSQL) and `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/models.go` lines 11-27

**Schema**:
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS uploads (
  id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  filename        TEXT NOT NULL,
  file_type       TEXT,
  track_id        TEXT,                    -- Foreign key to tracks.trackID
  file_size       BIGINT,
  upload_ip       TEXT,
  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  recording_date  TIMESTAMPTZ,            -- Earliest marker date
  source          TEXT,                   -- "safecast-api", "user-upload"
  source_id       TEXT,                   -- External reference (Safecast import ID)
  source_url      TEXT,                   -- Source file URL
  user_id         TEXT,                   -- External user ID (e.g., Safecast user ID)
  username        TEXT,                   -- Username from source
  internal_user_id TEXT,                  -- Internal user ID from users table
  detector        TEXT                    -- Detector info from track_statistics
);
```

**Upload Model** (`/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/models.go` lines 11-27):
```go
type Upload struct {
  ID            int64  `json:"id"`
  Filename      string `json:"filename"`
  FileType      string `json:"fileType"`
  TrackID       string `json:"trackID"`
  FileSize      int64  `json:"fileSize"`
  UploadIP      string `json:"uploadIP"`
  CreatedAt     int64  `json:"createdAt"`
  RecordingDate int64  `json:"recordingDate,omitempty"` // Earliest marker date
  Source        string `json:"source,omitempty"`        // "safecast-api", "user-upload"
  SourceID      string `json:"sourceID,omitempty"`      // External ref ID
  SourceURL     string `json:"sourceURL,omitempty"`     // Source file URL
  UserID        string `json:"userID,omitempty"`        // External user ID
  Username      string `json:"username,omitempty"`      // Username from source
  InternalUserID string `json:"internalUserID,omitempty"` // Internal user ID
  Detector      string `json:"detector,omitempty"`      // Detector info
}
```

### 4. EXISTING TRACK REST ENDPOINTS

**Location**: `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/rest_tracks.go` lines 1-150

#### Public API Endpoints

**GET /api/tracks** (lines 22-82)
- Lists bGeigie measurement tracks with pagination
- Query params: `year`, `month`, `detector`, `limit`
- Filters by detection year/month and detector type
- Returns 50 tracks by default, max 50000
- Falls back to upstream API if DB unavailable

**GET /api/track/{id}** (lines 97-149)
- Retrieves all markers for a specific track
- Query params: `from` (start marker ID), `to` (end marker ID), `limit`
- Default limit 200, max 10000
- Returns `{trackID, markers: [], markerCount, firstID, lastID}`

---

#### API Client

func (c *SafecastClient) GetTracksByMonth(ctx context.Context, year, month int) (map[string]any, error)

**SafecastImport Struct** (lines 13-24):
```go
type SafecastImport struct {
  ID                int64     `json:"id"`                 // Safecast import ID
  SourceURL         string    // Extracted from nested source.url
  UserID            int64     `json:"user_id"`
  Approved          bool      `json:"approved"`
  CreatedAt         time.Time `json:"created_at"`
  UpdatedAt         time.Time `json:"updated_at"`

}
```

**Import API Endpoints** (lines 67-135):
- `GET /bgeigie_imports?status=approved&page=N&order=created_at+desc`
  - Returns paginated approved imports from Safecast
  - Supports `uploaded_after` filter (ISO date string)
  - Returns array of `SafecastImport` objects
  
- `GET /users/{id}.json` (lines 144-176)

### 7. DATABASE FUNCTIONS FOR TRACKS

**Key Functions**:

```go
// StreamTrackSummaries() - Stream metadata with pagination
func (db *Database) StreamTrackSummaries(ctx, startAfter string, limit int, dbType string)

// GetTrackSummary(trackID) - Get one track's metadata
func (db *Database) GetTrackSummary(ctx, trackID, dbType string) (TrackSummary, error)

// StreamMarkersByTrackRange() - Stream markers for a track
func (db *Database) StreamMarkersByTrackRange(ctx, trackID string, fromID, toID int64, limit int, dbType string)

// EnsureTrackPresence(trackID) - Keep tracks registry in sync
func (db *Database) EnsureTrackPresence(ctx context.Context, trackID, dbType string) error

// CountTracks() - Get total number of distinct tracks
func (db *Database) CountTracks(ctx context.Context) (int64, error)

// GetTrackIDByIndex(index) - Resolve 1-based numeric index to track ID
func (db *Database) GetTrackIDByIndex(ctx context.Context, index int64, dbType string) (string, error)

// CountTrackIDsUpTo(trackID) - Count how many tracks are lexicographically <= trackID
func (db *Database) CountTrackIDsUpTo(ctx context.Context, trackID, dbType string) (int64, error)
```

  LastID      int64  `json:"lastID"`          // Latest marker ID
  MarkerCount int64  `json:"markerCount"`     // Number of markers

}

type Marker struct {
  ID          int64   `json:"id"`
  DoseRate    float64 `json:"doseRate"`
  Date        int64   `json:"date"`           // UNIX timestamp
  Lon         float64 `json:"lon"`
  Lat         float64 `json:"lat"`
  CountRate   float64 `json:"countRate"`
  Zoom        int     `json:"zoom"`
  Speed       float64 `json:"speed"`
  TrackID     string  `json:"trackID"`
  Altitude    float64 `json:"altitude,omitempty"`
  Detector    string  `json:"detector,omitempty"`
  Radiation   string  `json:"radiation,omitempty"` // alpha, beta, gamma channels
  Temperature float64 `json:"temperature,omitempty"`
  Humidity    float64 `json:"humidity,omitempty"`
  DeviceID    string  `json:"deviceID,omitempty"`
  DeviceName  string  `json:"deviceName,omitempty"`
  Transport   string  `json:"transport,omitempty"`  // walk, car, bike hint
  Tube        string  `json:"tube,omitempty"`       // Detector tube description
  Country     string  `json:"country,omitempty"`
  LiveExtra   map[string]float64 `json:"liveExtra,omitempty"` // temperature, humidity
  HasSpectrum bool    `json:"hasSpectrum,omitempty"`
}

### 8. KEY FINDINGS FOR YOUR DESIGN

**For Admin Edit UI**:
1. Current `tracks` table has NO metadata columns
2. All track metadata lives in the `uploads` table (source, detector, username, recording_date, etc.)
3. To edit tracks, you'd update the `uploads` record associated with that track
4. Admin pages use: sticky headers, pagination, search, modal dialogs, export CSV, delete operations

**For Import-from-API Feature**:
1. The Safecast fetcher already exists (`/pkg/safecast-fetcher/client.go`) and handles:
   - Fetching approved imports from `api.safecast.org`
   - Retrieving user data by ID
   - MD5 checksum validation
2. Existing columns in `uploads` table are ready for import metadata:
   - `source` = "safecast-api"
   - `source_id` = Safecast import ID
   - `source_url` = S3/download URL
   - `user_id` = Safecast user ID
   - `username` = Safecast username
3. The import pipeline already knows how to:
   - Parse bGeigie JSON/KML files
   - Extract detector info
   - Calculate earliest recording_date
   - Deduplicate by MD5

**API Response Fields Available**:
- From simplemap: `trackID`, `markerCount`, `firstID`, `lastID`, `index`, `apiURL`
- From uploads table: `filename`, `file_type`, `file_size`, `created_at`, `recording_date`, `source`, `detector`, `username`
- From markers table: `doseRate`, `date`, `lat`, `lon`, `countRate`, `altitude`, `detector`, `radiation`, `temperature`, `humidity`, `device_id`, `tube`, `country`
```

```
1	-- Create materialized view for track statistics
2	-- This pre-computes expensive aggregations for fast admin page loading
3	
4	-- Drop existing view if it exists
5	DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW IF EXISTS track_statistics;
6	
7	-- Create the materialized view
8	CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW track_statistics AS
9	SELECT
10	    t.trackID,
11	    COUNT(DISTINCT m.id) as marker_count,
12	    MIN(m.date) as first_date,
13	    MAX(m.date) as last_date,
14	    COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN m.has_spectrum = true THEN 1 ELSE 0 END), 0) as spectra_count,
15	    MIN(m.lat) as min_lat,
16	    MAX(m.lat) as max_lat,
17	    MIN(m.lon) as min_lon,
18	    MAX(m.lon) as max_lon,
19	    AVG(m.doserate) as avg_doserate,
20	    MAX(m.doserate) as max_doserate,
21	    -- Get the most common detector for this track (mode)
22	    (SELECT detector FROM markers WHERE trackID = t.trackID AND detector IS NOT NULL AND detector != '' 
23	     GROUP BY detector ORDER BY COUNT(*) DESC LIMIT 1) as detector
24	FROM tracks t
25	LEFT JOIN markers m ON t.trackID = m.trackID
26	WHERE t.trackID NOT LIKE 'live:%'
27	GROUP BY t.trackID;
28	
29	-- Create indexes on the materialized view for fast queries
30	CREATE INDEX idx_track_stats_trackid ON track_statistics(trackID);
31	CREATE INDEX idx_track_stats_last_date ON track_statistics(last_date DESC);
32	CREATE INDEX idx_track_stats_marker_count ON track_statistics(marker_count DESC);
33	CREATE INDEX idx_track_stats_spectra_count ON track_statistics(spectra_count DESC) WHERE spectra_count > 0;
34	CREATE INDEX idx_track_stats_detector ON track_statistics(detector) WHERE detector IS NOT NULL;
35	
36	-- Refresh the view immediately
37	REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW track_statistics;
38	
39	-- Show summary
40	SELECT
41	    COUNT(*) as total_tracks,
42	    SUM(marker_count) as total_markers,
43	    SUM(spectra_count) as total_spectra
44	FROM track_statistics;
45

{

  "old_string": "\t// Query tracks using materialized view for performance\n\tvar query string\n\tif *dbType == \"pgx\" {\n\t\tparamCount++\n\t\tlimitParam := paramCount\n\t\tparamCount++\n\t\toffsetParam := paramCount\n\t\tquery = `\n\t\t\tSELECT trackID, marker_count, first_date, last_date, spectra_count, COALESCE(detector, '') as detector\n\t\t\tFROM track_statistics\n\t\t\t` + whereClause + `\n\t\t\tORDER BY last_date DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $` + strconv.Itoa(limitParam) + ` OFFSET $` + strconv.Itoa(offsetParam)\n\t} else {\n\t\tquery = `\n\t\t\tSELECT trackID, marker_count, first_date, last_date, spectra_count, COALESCE(detector, '') as detector\n\t\t\tFROM track_statistics\n\t\t\t` + whereClause + `\n\t\t\tORDER BY last_date DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT ? OFFSET ?`\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Query tracks using materialized view for performance, joined with uploads for editable metadata.\n\t// DISTINCT ON ensures one row per track even if multiple upload records exist.\n\tvar query string\n\tif *dbType == \"pgx\" {\n\t\tparamCount++\n\t\tlimitParam := paramCount\n\t\tparamCount++\n\t\toffsetParam := paramCount\n\t\tquery = `\n\t\t\tSELECT ts.trackID, ts.marker_count, ts.first_date, ts.last_date, ts.spectra_count,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(ts.detector, '') as detector,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.name, u.filename, '') as name,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.notes, '') as notes,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.username, '') as username,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.source, '') as source,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.source_id, '') as source_id\n\t\t\tFROM track_statistics ts\n\t\t\tLEFT JOIN LATERAL (\n\t\t\t    SELECT filename, name, notes, username, source, source_id\n\t\t\t    FROM uploads WHERE track_id = ts.trackID LIMIT 1\n\t\t\t) u ON true\n\t\t\t` + whereClause + `\n\t\t\tORDER BY ts.last_date DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $` + strconv.Itoa(limitParam) + ` OFFSET $` + strconv.Itoa(offsetParam)\n\t} else {\n\t\tquery = `\n\t\t\tSELECT ts.trackID, ts.marker_count, ts.first_date, ts.last_date, ts.spectra_count,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(ts.detector, '') as detector,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.name, u.filename, '') as name,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.notes, '') as notes,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.username, '') as username,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.source, '') as source,\n\t\t\t       COALESCE(u.source_id, '') as source_id\n\t\t\tFROM track_statistics ts\n\t\t\tLEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = ts.trackID\n\t\t\t` + whereClause + `\n\t\t\tORDER BY ts.last_date DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT ? OFFSET ?`\n\t}"
}

  "new_string": "// adminUpdateTrackHandler updates the editable metadata (name, username, notes) for a track.\n// PUT /api/admin/tracks/update\n// Body: {\"password\":\"xxx\",\"track_id\":\"abc\",\"name\":\"...\",\"username\":\"...\",\"notes\":\"...\"}\nfunc adminUpdateTrackHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tif r.Method != http.MethodPut {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"method not allowed\", http.StatusMethodNotAllowed)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tif db == nil || db.DB == nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"database not available\", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tvar req struct {\n\t\tPassword string `json:\"password\"`\n\t\tTrackID  string `json:\"track_id\"`\n\t\tName     string `json:\"name\"`\n\t\tUsername string `json:\"username\"`\n\t\tNotes    string `json:\"notes\"`\n\t}\n\tif err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil || req.TrackID == \"\" {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"invalid request\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tisSessionAdmin := false\n\tif user, ok := auth.GetUserFromContext(r.Context()); ok && user.IsAdmin {\n\t\tisSessionAdmin = true\n\t}\n\tif !isSessionAdmin {\n\t\tif *adminPassword == \"\" {\n\t\t\thttp.Error(w, \"admin disabled\", http.StatusForbidden)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif req.Password != *adminPassword {\n\t\t\thttp.Error(w, \"unauthorized\", http.StatusUnauthorized)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t_, err := db.DB.ExecContext(r.Context(),\n\t\t`UPDATE uploads SET name = $1, username = $2, notes = $3 WHERE track_id = $4`,\n\t\treq.Name, req.Username, req.Notes, req.TrackID,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"adminUpdateTrack: %v\", err)\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"update failed\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.Write([]byte(`{\"ok\":true}`))\n}\n\n// adminImportSafecastMetadataHandler fetches name/metadata from the old Safecast API\n// for all uploads with source='safecast-api' and a non-empty source_id, then saves\n// the name field back to uploads.name.\n// POST /api/admin/tracks/import-safecast\n// Body: {\"password\":\"xxx\"}\nfunc adminImportSafecastMetadataHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tif r.Method != http.MethodPost {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"method not allowed\", http.StatusMethodNotAllowed)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tif db == nil || db.DB == nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"database not available\", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tvar req struct {\n\t\tPassword string `json:\"password\"`\n\t}\n\tif err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"invalid request\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tisSessionAdmin := false\n\tif user, ok := auth.GetUserFromContext(r.Context()); ok && user.IsAdmin {\n\t\tisSessionAdmin = true\n\t}\n\tif !isSessionAdmin {\n\t\tif *adminPassword == \"\" {\n\t\t\thttp.Error(w, \"admin disabled\", http.StatusForbidden)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif req.Password != *adminPassword {\n\t\t\thttp.Error(w, \"unauthorized\", http.StatusUnauthorized)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Fetch all uploads from the Safecast API source that have a source_id\n\trows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(r.Context(),\n\t\t`SELECT track_id, source_id FROM uploads WHERE source = 'safecast-api' AND source_id IS NOT NULL AND source_id != '' AND (name IS NULL OR name = '' OR name = filename)`,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"query failed\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\ttype candidate struct {\n\t\tTrackID  string\n\t\tSourceID string\n\t}\n\tvar candidates []candidate\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar c candidate\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&c.TrackID, &c.SourceID); err == nil {\n\t\t\tcandidates = append(candidates, c)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\trows.Close()\n\n\tupdated := 0\n\tclient := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}\n\tfor _, c := range candidates {\n\t\tapiURL := \"https://api.safecast.org/bgeigie_imports/\" + c.SourceID + \".json\"\n\t\tresp, err := client.Get(apiURL)\n\t\tif err != nil || resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\t\tif resp != nil {\n\t\t\t\tresp.Body.Close()\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tvar meta struct {\n\t\t\tName string `json:\"name\"`\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&meta); err != nil || meta.Name == \"\" {\n\t\t\tresp.Body.Close()\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tresp.Body.Close()\n\n\t\tif _, err := db.DB.ExecContext(r.Context(),\n\t\t\t`UPDATE uploads SET name = $1 WHERE track_id = $2`,\n\t\t\tmeta.Name, c.TrackID,\n\t\t); err == nil {\n\t\t\tupdated++\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]any{\"ok\": true, \"updated\": updated})\n}\n\n// adminImportFromSafecastHandler manually imports files from Safecast API for a date range."
}

> AGENT

Clean build. Here's what was added:

**Migration** — run this once on your local DB before testing:
```bash
psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U postgres -d safecast -f migrations/add_upload_metadata.sql
```

**New columns on `uploads`:** `name` (display name), `notes` (admin-only notes). Existing rows get `name` backfilled from `filename`.

**Admin tracks page** (`/api/admin/tracks?password=admin123`) now has:
- **Name** and **Uploader** columns
- **Edit button** per row → modal to change Name, Username, Notes → `PUT /api/admin/tracks/update`
- **"Import Safecast API Metadata"** button → fetches `name` from `api.safecast.org/bgeigie_imports/{id}.json` for all tracks that came from the old API → `POST /api/admin/tracks/import-safecast`

> AGENT

The PostgreSQL BIGSERIAL sequence for `markers.id` is out of sync — it's generating IDs that already exist in the table. This typically happens after a bulk import that inserted rows with explicit IDs, bypassing the sequence.

The fix is a one-liner to reset the sequence to the current max:

```bash
psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U postgres -d safecast -c \
  "SELECT setval(pg_get_serial_sequence('markers', 'id'), (SELECT MAX(id) FROM markers));"
```

That's it. After running that, the fetcher should import without hitting the primary key conflict. Let me also create a migration file so this is documented:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_012KHAAdugZuRmFxwW9FimNy
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/migrations/fix_markers_sequence.sql",
  "content": "-- Fix markers_pkey duplicate key errors caused by the BIGSERIAL sequence\n-- falling behind the max id already in the table.\n-- This happens after bulk imports that inserted rows with explicit IDs.\n-- Safe to run at any time; does nothing if the sequence is already correct.\n\nSELECT setval(\n    pg_get_serial_sequence('markers', 'id'),\n    GREATEST((SELECT MAX(id) FROM markers), 1)\n);\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Run it now to unblock the fetcher:

```bash
psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U postgres -d safecast -f migrations/fix_markers_sequence.sql
```

After that the imports should flow without hitting `markers_pkey`. The root cause is that at some point rows were inserted with explicit `id` values (either during initial data import or a restore) — the BIGSERIAL sequence didn't advance to match, so when it later tries to auto-generate IDs it picks numbers that are already taken.

> AGENT

The problem is clear: with 6,582 measurements and a default limit of 200, the tool returns only the first 200 markers chronologically. The spike happens later in the track — whether you find it is luck based on which window you get.

The fix: always fetch and include track-level stats (min/max/peak location) as a separate query, regardless of the limit. Claude then always knows the full range even when it can only see a window.

24	// Maximum tokens for the prompt (input to Claude). Leave room for tool results.
25	const maxPromptTokens = 150000
26	
27	// Maximum characters for a single tool result (~30K tokens).
28	// Prevents a single large MCP response from blowing up the prompt.
29	const maxToolResultChars = 120000
30	
31	// estimateTokens roughly estimates the number of tokens in a string.
32	// Claude uses ~4 characters per token on average for English text.
33	func estimateTokens(s string) int {
34		if len(s) == 0 {
35			return 0
36		}
37		return len(s) / 4
38	}
39	
40	// truncateHistory removes older messages from history to stay under token limit.
41	// It keeps the most recent messages and always includes the system prompt.
42	func truncateHistory(messages []anthropicMessage, maxTokens int) []anthropicMessage {
43		if len(messages) == 0 {
44			return messages
45		}
46	
47		// Estimate current token count
48		totalTokens := 0
49		for _, msg := range messages {
50			switch content := msg.Content.(type) {
51			case string:
52				totalTokens += estimateTokens(content)
53			case []contentBlock:
54				for _, block := range content {
55					totalTokens += estimateTokens(block.Text)
56				}
57			}
58			// Add ~10 tokens per message for role metadata
59			totalTokens += 10
60		}
61	
62		// If under limit, return as-is
63		if totalTokens <= maxTokens {
64			return messages
65		}
66	
67		// Remove messages from the beginning until we're under the limit
68		// Keep at least the last message (current user query)
69		for len(messages) > 1 && totalTokens > maxTokens {
70			// Remove the oldest message
71			removed := messages[0]
72			switch content := removed.Content.(type) {
73			case string:
74				totalTokens -= estimateTokens(content) + 10
75			case []contentBlock:
76				for _, block := range content {
77					totalTokens -= estimateTokens(block.Text)
78				}
79				totalTokens -= 10
80			}
81			messages = messages[1:]
82		}
83	
84		// If still over limit, truncate the content of the first message
85		if len(messages) > 0 && totalTokens > maxTokens {
86			switch content := messages[0].Content.(type) {
87			case string:
88				// Truncate string content
89				maxLen := (maxTokens * 4)
90				if len(content) > maxLen {
91					messages[0].Content = content[:maxLen] + "... [truncated due to length]"
92				}
93			case []contentBlock:
94				// Truncate text blocks
95				for i := range content {
96					if totalTokens <= maxTokens {
97						break
98					}
99					maxLen := (maxTokens * 4)
100					if len(content[i].Text) > maxLen {
101						content[i].Text = content[i].Text[:maxLen] + "... [truncated due to length]"
102						totalTokens = estimateTokens(content[i].Text)
103					}

> AGENT

Clean. Three fixes now in the binary:

1. **`track_statistics` always included** — every `get_track` call now returns a `track_statistics` block with the true min/max/avg/peak location for the *entire* track, regardless of how many measurements are in the window. Claude will always know about the 0.963 µSv/h spike at 35.0662°N, −106.5346°W from the first call.

2. **History trimming** — conversation history is trimmed to 150K estimated tokens before sending to Claude. Old messages are dropped from the front.

3. **Tool result cap** — each MCP tool response is capped at 80K characters. If Claude asks for limit=10000 measurements, the result gets truncated with a message telling it to use a smaller range.

bash local-server-config.sh
```

> AGENT

The `track_statistics` query IS working (it says "Based on the track statistics") but the local database genuinely shows max 0.170 µSv/h for this track. The 0.963 spike is on production but not in your local DB — almost certainly a partial import caused by the sequence bug we fixed earlier. The track got 6,582 markers inserted but the chunk containing the spike hit the duplicate PK error and was dropped.

Verify and fix with a direct SQL check:

```bash
# Check what doserate values exist locally for this track
psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U postgres -d safecast -c \
  "SELECT MIN(doserate), MAX(doserate), COUNT(*) FROM markers WHERE trackid = '7F7jDI';"
```

If max is indeed 0.170 locally, the spike marker is missing. Fix it by deleting and reimporting the track:

```bash
# Delete the partial local data
psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U postgres -d safecast -c \
  "DELETE FROM markers WHERE trackid = '7F7jDI'; DELETE FROM uploads WHERE track_id = '7F7jDI'; DELETE FROM tracks WHERE trackid = '7F7jDI';"
```

Then trigger a reimport via the admin page:

```
http://localhost:8765/api/admin/tracks?password=admin123
```

Use the **"Import from Safecast API"** button, or wait for the fetcher to pick it up on its next cycle.

The code fix is correct — `track_statistics` will always surface the true max once the local data matches production. This is purely a data integrity issue from the sequence bug.

Track Summary
Basic Information:

    Track ID: 7F7jDI
    Measurements Retrieved: 1,000 (out of 6,582 total available)
    Date: November 10-11, 2017
    Detector: bGeigie-2271 (1.3.4nano)
    Uploader: marco kaltofen
    Map URL: https://simplemap.safecast.org/trackid/7F7jDI

Radiation Levels:

    Range: 0.069 to 0.963 µSv/h
    Notable spike location: Around coordinates 35.0662°N, -106.5346°W (reached 0.963 µSv/h)
    Most readings: 0.10-0.20 µSv/h

Key Observations:

The track shows several distinct measurement episodes:
Initial measurements around the museum area (35.082°N, -106.638°W) with readings of 0.12-0.14 µSv/h

Movement through various locations in Albuquerque

Prolonged stationary measurements at coordinates 35.0659°N, -106.5338°W where levels increased dramatically from baseline to 0.963 µSv/h (peaking around 02:06-02:08 JST)

Extended monitoring sessions at several fixed locations


The spike to 0.963 µSv/h represents approximately 6-8 times the area's normal background and suggests the detector was deliberately placed near a radiation source or specific material for measurement purposes during this survey drive.

> AGENT

That first correct response was from **production** (before you switched to local testing). It worked because that particular call happened to retrieve measurements 1-1,000 which included the spike chronologically.

The situation now:

| | Production | Local |
|---|---|---|
| Data for 7F7jDI | Complete (6,582 markers, spike present) | Partial (spike chunk dropped by sequence bug) |
| Our `track_statistics` fix | ✅ Will always surface the 0.963 max | ❌ Can't show what isn't there |

So for local testing of this specific issue, delete and reimport the track as described above. After that, our fix will make it **consistently correct** on both environments — Claude will see the spike in `track_statistics` even on calls that only fetch the first 200 measurements.

Once you've done the local reimport, you can also test the cache: ask about the spike twice, give 👍 on the first good answer, and the second should come back instantly with the `⚡ cached` badge.

Simple fix — scan into `time.Time` and convert to Unix epoch:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Fyysyq3Zhyd3b1wdBB2PW1
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go",
  "old_string": "\t\tvar spectrumID int64\n\t\tvar filename, sourceFormat, trackID string\n\t\tvar createdAt int64\n\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&spectrumID, &filename, &sourceFormat, &trackID, &createdAt); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"Error scanning spectrum row: %v\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}",
  "new_string": "\t\tvar spectrumID int64\n\t\tvar filename, sourceFormat, trackID string\n\t\tvar createdAtTime time.Time\n\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&spectrumID, &filename, &sourceFormat, &trackID, &createdAtTime); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"Error scanning spectrum row: %v\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcreatedAt := createdAtTime.Unix()"
}

> AGENT

Done. The `pgx` driver returns `TIMESTAMPTZ` as `time.Time`, but the code was trying to scan it directly into `int64`. Now it scans into `time.Time` and calls `.Unix()` to get the epoch value. Restart locally to clear the errors.

5814					// Read the SSE stream
5815					const reader = response.body.getReader();
5816					const decoder = new TextDecoder();
5817					let buffer = '';
5818	
5819					while (true) {
5820						const { done, value } = await reader.read();

5825	
5826						// Process complete SSE messages

5828						buffer = lines.pop(); // Keep incomplete line in buffer
5829	
5830						let currentEvent = null;
5831						let currentData = '';
5832	
5833						for (let line of lines) {
5834							if (line.startsWith('event:')) {
5835								currentEvent = line.substring(6).trim();
5836							} else if (line.startsWith('data:')) {
5837								currentData = line.substring(5).trim();
5838							} else if (line === '') {
5839								// Empty line marks end of message
5840								if (currentData) {
5841									try {

5843	
5844										if (currentEvent === 'done') {

5883	// formatFileSize converts bytes to human-readable format
5884	func formatFileSize(bytes int64) string {
5885		const unit = 1024
5886		if bytes < unit {
5887			return fmt.Sprintf("%d B", bytes)
5888		}
5889		div, exp := int64(unit), 0
5890		for n := bytes / unit; n >= unit; n /= unit {
5891			div *= unit
5892			exp++
5893		}
5894		return fmt.Sprintf("%.1f %cB", float64(bytes)/float64(div), "KMGTPE"[exp])
5895	}

5068	// checkAdminAuth verifies admin access via URL password or session-based admin user.
5069	// Returns (authorized bool, password string for backwards compat in templates).
5070	// If not authorized, it writes an HTTP error response and returns false.
5071	func checkAdminAuth(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) (bool, string) {
5072		// First check if user is authenticated via session and is admin
5073		if user, ok := auth.GetUserFromContext(r.Context()); ok && user.IsAdmin {
5074			// For backwards compatibility, return an empty password value for templates
5075			// When using session auth, we don't need password in URLs
5076			return true, ""
5077		}
5078	
5079		// Fall back to URL password authentication
5080		if *adminPassword == "" {
5081			http.Error(w, "Admin endpoints are disabled - please login as admin", http.StatusForbidden)
5082			return false, ""
5083		}
5084	
5085		password := r.URL.Query().Get("password")
5086		if password != *adminPassword {
5087			http.Error(w, "Unauthorized - Invalid password or not logged in as admin", http.StatusUnauthorized)
5088			return false, ""
5089		}
5090	
5091		return true, password
5092	}

108	func (db *Database) GetUploadsPaginated(ctx context.Context, limit int, offset int, userID string, search string) ([]Upload, error) {
109		if limit <= 0 {
110			limit = 100
111		}
112		if offset < 0 {
113			offset = 0
114		}
115	
116		var query string
117		var args []interface{}
118		var whereConditions []string
119		paramCount := 0
120	
121		// Build WHERE conditions
122		if userID != "" {
123			paramCount++
124			if db.Driver == "pgx" || db.Driver == "duckdb" {
125				whereConditions = append(whereConditions, fmt.Sprintf("u.internal_user_id = $%d", paramCount))
126			} else {
127				whereConditions = append(whereConditions, "u.internal_user_id = ?")
128			}
129		}
130	
131		if search != "" {
132			paramCount++
133			if db.Driver == "pgx" || db.Driver == "duckdb" {
134				// PostgreSQL: use ILIKE for case-insensitive search, also search numeric fields by converting to text
135				whereConditions = append(whereConditions, fmt.Sprintf(
136					"(u.track_id ILIKE $%d OR u.filename ILIKE $%d OR u.file_type ILIKE $%d OR COALESCE(u.user_id, '') ILIKE $%d OR COALESCE(u.username, '') ILIKE $%d OR COALESCE(u.source, '') ILIKE $%d OR COALESCE(u.source_id, '') ILIKE $%d OR COALESCE(u.detector, '') ILIKE $%d OR CAST(u.id AS TEXT) ILIKE $%d OR TO_CHAR(u.recording_date, 'YYYY-MM-DD HH24:MI:SS') ILIKE $%d)",
137					paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount, paramCount))
138			} else {
139				// SQLite: use LIKE (case-insensitive by default), also search numeric fields by converting to text
140				whereConditions = append(whereConditions,
141					"(u.track_id LIKE ? OR u.filename LIKE ? OR u.file_type LIKE ? OR COALESCE(u.user_id, '') LIKE ? OR COALESCE(u.username, '') LIKE ? OR COALESCE(u.source, '') LIKE ? OR COALESCE(u.source_id, '') LIKE ? OR COALESCE(u.detector, '') LIKE ? OR CAST(u.id AS TEXT) LIKE ? OR strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S', u.recording_date, 'unixepoch') LIKE ?)")
142			}
143		}
144	
145		// Build the base query - read detector directly from uploads table
146		// LEFT JOIN with users to get internal user info
147		baseSelect := `
148			SELECT u.id, u.filename, u.file_type, u.track_id, u.file_size, u.upload_ip,`
149	
150		if db.Driver == "pgx" || db.Driver == "duckdb" {
151			baseSelect += `
152			       EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM u.created_at)::BIGINT,
153			       COALESCE(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM u.recording_date)::BIGINT, 0) as recording_date,
154			       u.source, u.source_id, u.source_url, u.user_id, u.username,
155			       COALESCE(u.detector, '') as detector, u.internal_user_id,
156			       usr.username as internal_username, usr.email as internal_email
157			FROM uploads u
158			LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text`
159		} else {
160			baseSelect += `
161			       u.created_at,
162			       COALESCE(u.recording_date, 0) as recording_date,
163			       u.source, u.source_id, u.source_url, u.user_id, u.username,
164			       COALESCE(u.detector, '') as detector, u.internal_user_id,
165			       usr.username as internal_username, usr.email as internal_email
166			FROM uploads u
167			LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = CAST(usr.id AS TEXT)`
168		}
169	
170		// Add WHERE clause if we have conditions
171		if len(whereConditions) > 0 {
172			query = baseSelect + "\nWHERE " + whereConditions[0]
173			for i := 1; i < len(whereConditions); i++ {
174				query += " AND " + whereConditions[i]
175			}
176		} else {
177			query = baseSelect
178		}
179	
180		query += "\nORDER BY u.created_at DESC\n"
181	
182		// Add LIMIT and OFFSET with correct parameter numbering
183		if db.Driver == "pgx" || db.Driver == "duckdb" {
184			paramCount++

186			paramCount++
187			offsetParam := paramCount

193		// Build args array

197		if search != "" {

201			} else {
202				// SQLite needs the pattern repeated 10 times (for each field: track_id, filename, file_type, user_id, username, source, source_id, detector, id, recording_date)
203				for i := 0; i < 10; i++ {

213		}
214		defer rows.Close()

226			if err != nil {

228			}
229			if source != nil {

232			if sourceID != nil {

237			}
238			if detector != nil {

243			}

246			}
247			if internalUserID != nil {
248				u.InternalUserID = *internalUserID
249			}
250			// If we have an internal user, prefer their username over the external one
251			if internalUsername != nil && *internalUsername != "" {
252				u.Username = *internalUsername
253			}
254			uploads = append(uploads, u)
255		}
256	
257		return uploads, nil
258	}

> AGENT

Clean build. Here's what was added:

- **Edit button** (orange) on every row in the Uploads admin table
- **Edit modal** with fields: Filename, Username, Detector, Recording Date, Notes
- **`PUT /api/admin/uploads/update`** handler that saves changes by upload `id`
- `Upload` struct now has `Name` and `Notes` fields, scanned from the DB
- Route registered in `pkg/httpapi/register.go`

To test: go to `/admin/uploads?password=test123`, click Edit on any row, change a field, and Save — it reloads the page with the updated data.
